### EAA-014 — Economical reconsideration scheduling

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S006; DATE: 1988; WORK: Plans and Resource-Bounded Practical Reasoning; VERSION: Computational Intelligence 4, 349–355; 30-page author-formatted manuscript; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S014; DATE: 1995-05; WORK: Provably Bounded-Optimal Agents; VERSION: JAIR 2, 575–609; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S015; DATE: 1991; WORK: Commitment and Effectiveness of Situated Agents; VERSION: IJCAI 1991 pp.82–88; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Fixed commitment/reconsideration strategies compared under varied thinking cost and environmental change.

**Development:** Retain a guarded scheduling problem, not permanent reflection or a fixed universal depth.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain a guarded scheduling problem, not permanent reflection or a fixed universal depth.; OPERATION: Choose reconsideration opportunities using environmental change, action horizon, planning cost and failure consequence; use event triggers or cheap screens where sufficient.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Keep the current plan when new information cannot change its adequacy; use a fixed schedule only where justified by the setting.

**Problem:** Replanning on every event can cost more than its expected benefit, whereas never replanning ignores consequential change.

**Mechanism:** Choose reconsideration opportunities using environmental change, action horizon, planning cost and failure consequence; use event triggers or cheap screens where sufficient.

**Trigger and context:** Changing options or state during a persistent plan.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A working continuation policy and some discrimination between relevant and irrelevant changes.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Deliberation scheduler

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Deliberation scheduler; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A working continuation policy and some discrimination between relevant and irrelevant changes.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Clarify changed task meaning and report abandonment/non-fulfilment to the legitimate consumer where consequential. Consumer and prerequisite: Deliberation scheduler. A working continuation policy and some discrimination between relevant and irrelevant changes.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Reconsideration can alter a decision when worthwhile without automatically consuming every available action window.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Keep the current plan when new information cannot change its adequacy; use a fixed schedule only where justified by the setting.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Tileworld reversals depend on deliberation cost and change rate; there is no empirically universal reconsideration frequency.; Introduced cost or harm: Opportunity cost of reasoning and risk of delayed response.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Tileworld reversals depend on deliberation cost and change rate; there is no empirically universal reconsideration frequency.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C003; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S015; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Tileworld reversals depend on deliberation cost and change rate; there is no empirically universal reconsideration frequency.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S015; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Value-of-reconsideration estimates can themselves be expensive and uncertain.

**Unresolved questions:** When would another planning step be less useful than acting on the current adequate plan?; Value-of-reconsideration estimates can themselves be expensive and uncertain.

### EAA-015 — Retirement of obsolete, unreachable or exhausted goals

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S052; DATE: 1990-05-01; WORK: On Acting Together; VERSION: SRI Technical Note 485, submitted to AAAI 1990; scanned author report; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S051; DATE: 2012-07-24; WORK: Maintenance Goals in Intelligent Agents; VERSION: Accepted author manuscript; final Computational Intelligence 30(1), 71–114 issue 2014; DOI 10.1111/coin.12000; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S020; DATE: 2002; WORK: Towards Adjustable Autonomy for the Real World; VERSION: JAIR 17, 171–228; author manuscript paginated 1–58; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Persistent-goal termination on achievement, impossibility or loss of relevance; later resource-sensitive failure examples.

**Development:** Termination is evidence- and authority-sensitive, with explicit uncertainty rather than a success claim after giving up.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Termination is evidence- and authority-sensitive, with explicit uncertainty rather than a success claim after giving up.; OPERATION: Use relevance, feasibility, progress and budget evidence to suspend or retire pursuit; distinguish failure of one plan from impossibility of the goal and report material non-fulfilment.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Continue without a new retirement procedure when existing bounded execution already enforces the stop condition.

**Problem:** An agent may keep retrying an impossible task, consume resources or continue after the user’s purpose changes.

**Mechanism:** Use relevance, feasibility, progress and budget evidence to suspend or retire pursuit; distinguish failure of one plan from impossibility of the goal and report material non-fulfilment.

**Trigger and context:** Changed task, repeated unproductive attempts, exhausted resources or established infeasibility.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A legitimate goal source, evidence of progress/failure and an executable stop or handback path.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Goal manager and delegator

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Goal manager and delegator; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A legitimate goal source, evidence of progress/failure and an executable stop or handback path.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Clarify changed task meaning and report abandonment/non-fulfilment to the legitimate consumer where consequential. Consumer and prerequisite: Goal manager and delegator. A legitimate goal source, evidence of progress/failure and an executable stop or handback path.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** An obsolete or unsupported pursuit ceases rather than silently exhausting the environment or resources.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Continue without a new retirement procedure when existing bounded execution already enforces the stop condition.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Premature abandonment loses achievable goals; a wrong model can label a feasible task impossible.; Introduced cost or harm: Lost opportunities and human rework.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Premature abandonment loses achievable goals; a wrong model can label a feasible task impossible.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C003; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S051; EAA-S020; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Premature abandonment loses achievable goals; a wrong model can label a feasible task impossible.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S051; EAA-S020; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Impossibility beliefs remain fallible; “not achievable by this plan/budget” need not mean impossible in general.

**Unresolved questions:** Which evidence distinguishes a failed attempt from a goal that should no longer be pursued?; Impossibility beliefs remain fallible; “not achievable by this plan/budget” need not mean impossible in general.

The functional evidence is: An obsolete or unsupported pursuit ceases rather than silently exhausting the environment or resources.

<a id="eaa-016"></a>
### EAA-016 — Direct reactive control as a legitimate architecture branch

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S004; DATE: 1985-09; WORK: A Robust Layered Control System for a Mobile Robot; VERSION: MIT AI Memo 864; antecedent of 1986 journal article; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S005; DATE: 1991; WORK: Intelligence without representation; VERSION: Artificial Intelligence 47, 139–159; author reformatted copy; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S016; DATE: 1987; WORK: An Investigation into Reactive Planning in Complex Domains; VERSION: AAAI 1987 pp.202–206; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Task-achieving asynchronous behaviour layers and reactive action methods.

**Development:** Retain reactive control as an alternative and possible subsystem, not a primitive stage everyone must outgrow.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain reactive control as an alternative and possible subsystem, not a primitive stage everyone must outgrow.; OPERATION: Couple task-relevant observations to behaviours with explicit arbitration or inhibition where needed; let the world provide feedback instead of reconstructing it gratuitously.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: A fixed control rule can be the complete solution; add state or planning only for demonstrated aliasing, conflict or horizon needs.

**Problem:** Central modelling and planning can be too slow or unnecessary for directly observable local responses.

**Mechanism:** Couple task-relevant observations to behaviours with explicit arbitration or inhibition where needed; let the world provide feedback instead of reconstructing it gratuitously.

**Trigger and context:** Tight response deadlines, adequate local observability and behaviour-level objectives.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: Observable cues reliably connected to adequate actions and manageable interaction among behaviours.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Controller designer and runtime action selector

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Controller designer and runtime action selector; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Observable cues reliably connected to adequate actions and manageable interaction among behaviours.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Override, handback and authority changes must have real operational effects across active layers. Consumer and prerequisite: Controller designer and runtime action selector. Observable cues reliably connected to adequate actions and manageable interaction among behaviours.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** The agent responds effectively within the action window without depending on avoidable deliberation.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A fixed control rule can be the complete solution; add state or planning only for demonstrated aliasing, conflict or horizon needs.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Pure reaction can fail at delayed consequences, hidden state or non-local goals; suppression among behaviours can conceal priorities.; Introduced cost or harm: Limited foresight and behavioural interaction debugging.

### EAA-017 — Model-based deliberation when anticipation earns its cost

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S011; DATE: 1971; WORK: STRIPS: A New Approach to the Application of Theorem Proving to Problem Solving; VERSION: Artificial Intelligence 2, 189–208; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S012; DATE: 1998; WORK: Planning and Acting in Partially Observable Stochastic Domains; VERSION: Artificial Intelligence 101, 99–134; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S045; DATE: 1996; WORK: Planning-Based Control of Software Agents; VERSION: AIPS 1996 proceedings, pp.268–274; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Search over represented operator conditions and effects, later including uncertain belief-state decisions.

**Development:** Retain bounded model-based anticipation coupled to execution, not planning as a sufficient account of agency.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain bounded model-based anticipation coupled to execution, not planning as a sufficient account of agency.; OPERATION: Search or evaluate action sequences using explicit conditions, effects and objectives; expose uncertainty and pass plans to monitored execution rather than treating proof of a plan as fulfilment.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Use a validated fixed plan, policy or reactive response when search adds no useful discrimination.

**Problem:** Local reaction may not find a feasible sequence or account for delayed and uncertain consequences.

**Mechanism:** Search or evaluate action sequences using explicit conditions, effects and objectives; expose uncertainty and pass plans to monitored execution rather than treating proof of a plan as fulfilment.

**Trigger and context:** Non-local goals, dependencies or contingencies that a cheap direct policy cannot adequately handle.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: Adequate action/state model, a defined objective and computation available before the action opportunity closes.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Planner and executor

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Planner and executor; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Adequate action/state model, a defined objective and computation available before the action opportunity closes.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Override, handback and authority changes must have real operational effects across active layers. Consumer and prerequisite: Planner and executor. Adequate action/state model, a defined objective and computation available before the action opportunity closes.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** A selected plan is justified under stated assumptions and executable capabilities, with remaining model uncertainty visible.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Use a validated fixed plan, policy or reactive response when search adds no useful discrimination.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Model omission, computational expense and state change undermine practical benefit; original STRIPS already acknowledges execution issues, so “planning never monitors” is a caricature.; Introduced cost or harm: Search cost, stale state and model-maintenance effort.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Model omission, computational expense and state change undermine practical benefit; original STRIPS already acknowledges execution issues, so “planning never monitors” is a caricature.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C002; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S005; EAA-S014; EAA-S022; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Guarantees are relative to model adequacy and search approximation.

**Unresolved questions:** Which failure of the cheaper policy requires look-ahead, and is the model adequate for it?; Guarantees are relative to model adequacy and search approximation.

### EAA-018 — Executable BDI and plan-library interpretation

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S008; DATE: 1991-02; WORK: Modeling Rational Agents within a BDI-Architecture; VERSION: Australian AI Institute Technical Note 14; KR 1991 antecedent; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S009; DATE: 1995; WORK: BDI Agents: From Theory to Practice; VERSION: ICMAS 1995 pp.312–319; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S028; DATE: 2007; WORK: On the Formal Semantics of Speech-Act Based Communication in an Agent-Oriented Programming Language; VERSION: JAIR 29, 221–267; arXiv upload 2011; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** BDI abstract interpreters and practical plan-library approximations.

**Development:** Retain BDI as a family of practical choices with documented abstraction gaps, not a guarantee produced by naming three stores.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain BDI as a family of practical choices with documented abstraction gaps, not a guarantee produced by naming three stores.; OPERATION: Maintain relevant beliefs, goals/events and adopted intentions; select applicable library plans and update or drop them through explicit interpreter rules.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: A simpler task state machine or direct workflow can suffice when intention filtering and alternative plans add no value.

**Problem:** Generic reasoning may be too expensive, while a library of context-sensitive plans needs principled goal selection and commitment.

**Mechanism:** Maintain relevant beliefs, goals/events and adopted intentions; select applicable library plans and update or drop them through explicit interpreter rules.

**Trigger and context:** Recurrent structured tasks with usable plan recipes and persistent goals.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: Grounded belief updates, plan applicability conditions, event handling and commitment semantics.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Interpreter designer and goal manager

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Interpreter designer and goal manager; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Grounded belief updates, plan applicability conditions, event handling and commitment semantics.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Override, handback and authority changes must have real operational effects across active layers. Consumer and prerequisite: Interpreter designer and goal manager. Grounded belief updates, plan applicability conditions, event handling and commitment semantics.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** The implemented interpreter’s choices and updates can be related to its stated BDI abstractions.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A simpler task state machine or direct workflow can suffice when intention filtering and alternative plans add no value.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Logical closure, atomicity and infinite reasoning assumptions do not automatically survive an asynchronous finite implementation.; Introduced cost or harm: Library maintenance, event/goal interaction and interpreter complexity.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Logical closure, atomicity and infinite reasoning assumptions do not automatically survive an asynchronous finite implementation.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C002; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S009; EAA-S010; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Logical closure, atomicity and infinite reasoning assumptions do not automatically survive an asynchronous finite implementation.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S009; EAA-S010; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Plan-library coverage and event semantics matter; BDI labels do not identify one universal architecture.

**Unresolved questions:** What operational behaviour does the BDI structure add beyond a simpler equivalent controller?; Plan-library coverage and event semantics matter; BDI labels do not identify one universal architecture.

### EAA-019 — Hybrid arbitration and interface coherence

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S004; DATE: 1985-09; WORK: A Robust Layered Control System for a Mobile Robot; VERSION: MIT AI Memo 864; antecedent of 1986 journal article; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S046; DATE: 1995; WORK: Experiences with an Architecture for Intelligent, Reactive Agents; VERSION: ATAL 1995 workshop author copy; 17 pages; distinct later journal version; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** A planning/sequencing/skill-management architecture with continuous/discrete interfaces.

**Development:** Retain guarded integration with separately justified layers and test their interaction, not a mandatory three-tier pattern.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain guarded integration with separately justified layers and test their interaction, not a mandatory three-tier pattern.; OPERATION: Define how plan requests, skill activations, completion events and overrides cross layers; arbitrate concurrent demands and preserve relevant state/goal meaning across continuous and discrete interfaces.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Omit an unneeded layer; one or two tiers may satisfy the task and avoid integration costs.

**Problem:** Deliberation and fast control can each be adequate alone yet interfere through inconsistent state, priorities or timing.

**Mechanism:** Define how plan requests, skill activations, completion events and overrides cross layers; arbitrate concurrent demands and preserve relevant state/goal meaning across continuous and discrete interfaces.

**Trigger and context:** Combining reactive control with sequencing, deliberation or learning components.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: Explicit interface semantics, priority/override rules and observable execution outcomes.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Architecture integrator and executor

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Architecture integrator and executor; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Explicit interface semantics, priority/override rules and observable execution outcomes.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Override, handback and authority changes must have real operational effects across active layers. Consumer and prerequisite: Architecture integrator and executor. Explicit interface semantics, priority/override rules and observable execution outcomes.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Each active behaviour has a coherent relation to current plan and constraints, including during interruption or failure.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Omit an unneeded layer; one or two tiers may satisfy the task and avoid integration costs.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** 3T’s requested skill frequency is not a hard real-time guarantee; hybrids inherit coordination and modelling costs rather than automatically combining only strengths.; Introduced cost or harm: Coordination overhead, races and duplicated state.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: 3T’s requested skill frequency is not a hard real-time guarantee; hybrids inherit coordination and modelling costs rather than automatically combining only strengths.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C002; EAA-C005; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S022; EAA-S046; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Continuous/discrete assumptions, execution rates and atomicity must actually fit the implementation.

**Unresolved questions:** Which interface failure can arise only from composing these otherwise adequate components?; Continuous/discrete assumptions, execution rates and atomicity must actually fit the implementation.

### EAA-020 — Smallest adequate architecture and credible simpler baseline

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S005; DATE: 1991; WORK: Intelligence without representation; VERSION: Artificial Intelligence 47, 139–159; author reformatted copy; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S046; DATE: 1995; WORK: Experiences with an Architecture for Intelligent, Reactive Agents; VERSION: ATAL 1995 workshop author copy; 17 pages; distinct later journal version; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S035; DATE: 2024-07-01; WORK: AI Agents That Matter; VERSION: arXiv:2407.01502v1; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S036; DATE: 2024-10-29; WORK: Agentless: Demystifying LLM-based Software Engineering Agents; VERSION: arXiv:2407.01489v2; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Rival reactive, deliberative and hybrid designs; later empirical comparisons with simple retries and constrained workflows.

**Development:** Retain minimal adequacy as a selection discipline, not a universal preference for fewer components irrespective of consequences.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain minimal adequacy as a selection discipline, not a universal preference for fewer components irrespective of consequences.; OPERATION: Compare candidate configurations with a credible cheaper policy or fixed workflow under comparable tasks, resources and constraints; retain only complexity that earns its role.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Keep an existing adequate simpler mechanism; do not introduce an agent merely to satisfy a label.

**Problem:** Adding planners, memory, reflection or autonomous branching can be rewarded as sophistication without showing task benefit.

**Mechanism:** Compare candidate configurations with a credible cheaper policy or fixed workflow under comparable tasks, resources and constraints; retain only complexity that earns its role.

**Trigger and context:** Architecture selection, adding a module or claiming comparative superiority.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A task-relevant evaluation, resource accounting and a baseline with a genuine chance to succeed.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Architecture decision maker and evaluator

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Architecture decision maker and evaluator; BOUNDARY: This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A task-relevant evaluation, resource accounting and a baseline with a genuine chance to succeed.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Override, handback and authority changes must have real operational effects across active layers. Consumer and prerequisite: Architecture decision maker and evaluator. A task-relevant evaluation, resource accounting and a baseline with a genuine chance to succeed.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** The selected architecture has a stated reason to dominate or complement its cheaper alternatives in the declared setting.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Keep an existing adequate simpler mechanism; do not introduce an agent merely to satisfy a label.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** One successful fixed workflow does not show all autonomy is unnecessary; matching compute can change comparisons and long-horizon tasks may genuinely need adaptation.; Introduced cost or harm: Evaluation effort and the risk of underengineering rare consequential cases.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: One successful fixed workflow does not show all autonomy is unnecessary; matching compute can change comparisons and long-horizon tasks may genuinely need adaptation.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C002; EAA-C011; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S035; EAA-S036; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: One successful fixed workflow does not show all autonomy is unnecessary; matching compute can change comparisons and long-horizon tasks may genuinely need adaptation.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S035; EAA-S036; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Baseline adequacy, task distribution and resource matching are substantive assumptions.

**Unresolved questions:** What does the extra architectural freedom improve after comparison with the strongest affordable simpler alternative?; Baseline adequacy, task distribution and resource matching are substantive assumptions.

**Property versus ceremony:** CEREMONY_ID: EAA-CE020; PROPERTY_ID: EAA-020; ARTEFACT_RITUAL_OR_BRAND: Rival reactive, deliberative and hybrid designs; later empirical comparisons with simple retries and constrained workflows.; PROTECTED_PROPERTY_AND_FAILURE: Adding planners, memory, reflection or autonomous branching can be rewarded as sophistication without showing task benefit.; CONSUMER: Architecture decision maker and evaluator; PREREQUISITES: A task-relevant evaluation, resource accounting and a baseline with a genuine chance to succeed.; MINIMAL_ADEQUATE_ALTERNATIVE: Keep an existing adequate simpler mechanism; do not introduce an agent merely to satisfy a label.; TRIGGER_FOR_FULLER_FORM: Architecture selection, adding a module or claiming comparative superiority.; COSTS_OF_SIMPLIFICATION: Omitting this mechanism can reintroduce the specific failure: Adding planners, memory, reflection or autonomous branching can be rewarded as sophistication without showing task benefit. Simplification is acceptable only while the cheaper path still establishes the relevant criterion.; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT_CONDITIONS: Omit or retire a dedicated implementation when this trigger no longer applies, its consumer disappears, or an existing cheaper mechanism demonstrably establishes the same criterion. Do not erase relevant genealogy or unresolved effects. The functional evidence is: The selected architecture has a stated reason to dominate or complement its cheaper alternatives in the declared setting.

<a id="eaa-021"></a>
### EAA-021 — Ground actions in executable capabilities and affordances

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S011; DATE: 1971; WORK: STRIPS: A New Approach to the Application of Theorem Proving to Problem Solving; VERSION: Artificial Intelligence 2, 189–208; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S045; DATE: 1996; WORK: Planning-Based Control of Software Agents; VERSION: AIPS 1996 proceedings, pp.268–274; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S049; DATE: 2022-08-16; WORK: Do As I Can, Not As I Say: Grounding Language in Robotic Affordances; VERSION: arXiv:2204.01691v2; initial submission April 2022; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S039; DATE: 2026-08-23; WORK: Physical Agentic AI: An Architecture for Orchestrating a Robot Crew with LLMs; VERSION: arXiv:2608.22657v1; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Operators and tool/skill schemas, later language scores coupled with learned robot affordances.

**Development:** Ground both action identity and context-sensitive feasibility while retaining separate authority and effect checks.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Ground both action identity and context-sensitive feasibility while retaining separate authority and effect checks.; OPERATION: Map proposed actions to actual capability interfaces and task-relevant feasibility estimates; reject invented actions and distinguish a skill’s availability from its likely success.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: A fixed typed action set is enough where availability and effects are already established; learned affordance scoring is not mandatory.

**Problem:** A plan may name an action the executor cannot perform, or assign it an effect unsupported by the available skill.

**Mechanism:** Map proposed actions to actual capability interfaces and task-relevant feasibility estimates; reject invented actions and distinguish a skill’s availability from its likely success.

**Trigger and context:** Planning with tools, robots or dynamically available capabilities.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A known executor interface, applicable skill definitions and evidence about relevant conditions and effects.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Planner, tool adapter and executor

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Planner, tool adapter and executor; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A known executor interface, applicable skill definitions and evidence about relevant conditions and effects.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Report material failure or unresolved effects; seek permission for genuine task changes and provide feasible handback. Consumer and prerequisite: Planner, tool adapter and executor. A known executor interface, applicable skill definitions and evidence about relevant conditions and effects.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Every dispatched action denotes an available capability with interpretable parameters and a justified relation to the plan.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A fixed typed action set is enough where availability and effects are already established; learned affordance scoring is not mandatory.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** SayCan’s skill grounding improves discrimination in tested kitchens but does not close the planning/execution gap; capability does not confer permission.; Introduced cost or harm: Skill modelling, calibration, interface maintenance and rejected useful improvisation.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: SayCan’s skill grounding improves discrimination in tested kitchens but does not close the planning/execution gap; capability does not confer permission.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C006; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S049; EAA-S039; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: SayCan’s skill grounding improves discrimination in tested kitchens but does not close the planning/execution gap; capability does not confer permission.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S049; EAA-S039; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Feasibility estimates can be wrong and skill libraries incomplete; software-to-physical transfer requires new validation.

**Unresolved questions:** What ensures that the selected action actually exists and can plausibly have the intended effect here?; Feasibility estimates can be wrong and skill libraries incomplete; software-to-physical transfer requires new validation.

<a id="eaa-022"></a>
### EAA-022 — Recheck material action conditions at dispatch

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S011; DATE: 1971; WORK: STRIPS: A New Approach to the Application of Theorem Proving to Problem Solving; VERSION: Artificial Intelligence 2, 189–208; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S016; DATE: 1987; WORK: An Investigation into Reactive Planning in Complex Domains; VERSION: AAAI 1987 pp.202–206; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S039; DATE: 2026-08-23; WORK: Physical Agentic AI: An Architecture for Orchestrating a Robot Crew with LLMs; VERSION: arXiv:2608.22657v1; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Context-conditioned reactive methods and action applicability, later native runtime enforcement of generated plans.

**Development:** Retain action-time validation with honest sensing limits, not a universal gate that supposedly eliminates every unknown risk.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain action-time validation with honest sensing limits, not a universal gate that supposedly eliminates every unknown risk.; OPERATION: At the consequential dispatch boundary, check the still-relevant feasibility, state and delegated constraints; reject, refresh or refer invalid requests rather than relying only on earlier planning.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Use existing type/actuator checks or an atomic constrained operation when these already establish the required conditions.

**Problem:** A plan valid when selected can become invalid before execution, or a generated command can bypass relevant conditions.

**Mechanism:** At the consequential dispatch boundary, check the still-relevant feasibility, state and delegated constraints; reject, refresh or refer invalid requests rather than relying only on earlier planning.

**Trigger and context:** Mutable conditions, learned/generated plans, significant delays or consequential actions.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: Inspectable material conditions, a real interception point and an available outcome for failed checks.; PROPERTY_IDS: EAA-004; EAA-006; EAA-021; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Executor or capability boundary

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Executor or capability boundary; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Inspectable material conditions, a real interception point and an available outcome for failed checks.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Report material failure or unresolved effects; seek permission for genuine task changes and provide feasible handback. Consumer and prerequisite: Executor or capability boundary. Inspectable material conditions, a real interception point and an available outcome for failed checks.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** No action is knowingly dispatched on a material failed check; residual unobservable conditions are not represented as verified.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Use existing type/actuator checks or an atomic constrained operation when these already establish the required conditions.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Checks based on stale or omitted state can pass incorrectly; the 2026 simulation’s zero observed false dispatch does not establish universal safety.; Introduced cost or harm: Delay, false blocking and dependency on the checking mechanism.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Checks based on stale or omitted state can pass incorrectly; the 2026 simulation’s zero observed false dispatch does not establish universal safety.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C005; EAA-C006; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S022; EAA-S039; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Checks based on stale or omitted state can pass incorrectly; the 2026 simulation’s zero observed false dispatch does not establish universal safety.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S022; EAA-S039; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** A check-to-use race remains unless timing/atomicity is adequate; unmodelled preconditions are not automatically detected.

**Unresolved questions:** Which conditions must still hold when the action begins, and where are they actually checked?; A check-to-use race remains unless timing/atomicity is adequate; unmodelled preconditions are not automatically detected.

<a id="eaa-023"></a>
### EAA-023 — Distinguish issuance, acknowledgement and established effect

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S016; DATE: 1987; WORK: An Investigation into Reactive Planning in Complex Domains; VERSION: AAAI 1987 pp.202–206; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S022; DATE: 1999; WORK: Validating the DS1 Remote Agent Experiment; VERSION: ISAIRAS 1999 primary experiment report; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S032; DATE: 2024; WORK: WebArena: A Realistic Web Environment for Building Autonomous Agents; VERSION: arXiv:2307.13854v4; initial submission 2023; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S033; DATE: 2024-06; WORK: τ-bench: A Benchmark for Tool-Agent-User Interaction in Real-World Domains; VERSION: arXiv:2406.12045v1; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Execution monitors and result/state checks distinguish intended effects from command issue.

**Development:** Retain evidence-proportionate completion semantics and explicitly unresolved outcomes, not ritual double-checking of every operation.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain evidence-proportionate completion semantics and explicitly unresolved outcomes, not ritual double-checking of every operation.; OPERATION: Interpret acknowledgement according to its actual semantics; observe the task-relevant effect or retain uncertainty when it cannot be established, and use that result in subsequent action and reporting.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: A trustworthy atomic operation with a sufficient return contract may establish the effect directly; do not require redundant observation.

**Problem:** A sent command or success-looking tool response can be mistaken for the intended external change.

**Mechanism:** Interpret acknowledgement according to its actual semantics; observe the task-relevant effect or retain uncertainty when it cannot be established, and use that result in subsequent action and reporting.

**Trigger and context:** Actions with asynchronous, fallible, delayed or externally mediated effects.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A defined desired effect, a credible observation or return contract and a consumer of the result.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Executor, completion reporter and next action selector

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Executor, completion reporter and next action selector; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A defined desired effect, a credible observation or return contract and a consumer of the result.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Report material failure or unresolved effects; seek permission for genuine task changes and provide feasible handback. Consumer and prerequisite: Executor, completion reporter and next action selector. A defined desired effect, a credible observation or return contract and a consumer of the result.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** The system’s completion claim matches the strongest established consequence rather than its own action request.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A trustworthy atomic operation with a sufficient return contract may establish the effect directly; do not require redundant observation.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Independent observations can also fail; a missing acknowledgement does not prove no effect, making retries hazardous.; Introduced cost or harm: Observation cost, latency and possible duplicate side effects if uncertainty is mishandled.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Independent observations can also fail; a missing acknowledgement does not prove no effect, making retries hazardous.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C005; EAA-C006; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S022; EAA-S033; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Independent observations can also fail; a missing acknowledgement does not prove no effect, making retries hazardous.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S022; EAA-S033; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Observation sufficiency is task-specific; transport acknowledgement and application effect are not universally equivalent.

**Unresolved questions:** What establishes the intended change, and what does the current acknowledgement actually guarantee?; Observation sufficiency is task-specific; transport acknowledgement and application effect are not universally equivalent.

Simplification is acceptable only while the cheaper path still establishes the relevant criterion.; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT_CONDITIONS: Omit or retire a dedicated implementation when this trigger no longer applies, its consumer disappears, or an existing cheaper mechanism demonstrably establishes the same criterion. Do not erase relevant genealogy or unresolved effects. Specific non-trigger: A trustworthy atomic operation with a sufficient return contract may establish the effect directly; do not require redundant observation.; CONTROL_COST: Observation cost, latency and possible duplicate side effects if uncertainty is mishandled.; ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY: The presence of an artefact, module, prompt or meeting is insufficient. The functional evidence is: The system’s completion claim matches the strongest established consequence rather than its own action request.

### EAA-024 — Bounded contingency handling and plan repair

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S016; DATE: 1987; WORK: An Investigation into Reactive Planning in Complex Domains; VERSION: AAAI 1987 pp.202–206; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S022; DATE: 1999; WORK: Validating the DS1 Remote Agent Experiment; VERSION: ISAIRAS 1999 primary experiment report; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S030; DATE: 2023; WORK: ReAct: Synergizing Reasoning and Acting in Language Models; VERSION: arXiv:2210.03629v3; initial submission 2022; ICLR 2023 work; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S031; DATE: 2023-10-10; WORK: Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning; VERSION: arXiv:2303.11366v4; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Alternative reactive methods, monitoring, contingency choice and plan revision.

**Development:** Retain failure-sensitive repair with explicit stopping and escalation, not retry count as a proxy for persistence or competence.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain failure-sensitive repair with explicit stopping and escalation, not retry count as a proxy for persistence or competence.; OPERATION: Use observed failure to choose an applicable alternative, repair the plan or hand back; bound repetition by progress evidence, remaining resources and irreversible consequences.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Use a known safe retry for a transient failure when its conditions are established; stop directly when no useful recovery exists.

**Problem:** A single failed step can invalidate the rest of a plan, while unbounded retries consume resources without addressing the cause.

**Mechanism:** Use observed failure to choose an applicable alternative, repair the plan or hand back; bound repetition by progress evidence, remaining resources and irreversible consequences.

**Trigger and context:** Execution failure, changed conditions or a detected unmet effect.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: Failure information, alternative capabilities or an exit path, and preservation of goal and authority constraints.; PROPERTY_IDS: EAA-022; EAA-023; EAA-026; EAA-069; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Executor, planner and supervising participant

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Executor, planner and supervising participant; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Failure information, alternative capabilities or an exit path, and preservation of goal and authority constraints.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Report material failure or unresolved effects; seek permission for genuine task changes and provide feasible handback. Consumer and prerequisite: Executor, planner and supervising participant. Failure information, alternative capabilities or an exit path, and preservation of goal and authority constraints.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Recovery either produces new progress evidence or terminates with an accurate unresolved/failure status.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Use a known safe retry for a transient failure when its conditions are established; stop directly when no useful recovery exists.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Reflection can repeat the same error; a failure may stem from a wrong goal, sensor or oracle rather than the chosen action.; Introduced cost or harm: Delay, additional actions, accumulated side effects and repair complexity.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Reflection can repeat the same error; a failure may stem from a wrong goal, sensor or oracle rather than the chosen action.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C006; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S020; EAA-S031; EAA-S039; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Reflection can repeat the same error; a failure may stem from a wrong goal, sensor or oracle rather than the chosen action.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S020; EAA-S031; EAA-S039; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Retry safety and failure attribution are material; the environment may not reset and compensation may not restore the original state.

**Unresolved questions:** What new evidence makes another attempt worthwhile, and what stops ineffective recovery?; Retry safety and failure attribution are material; the environment may not reset and compensation may not restore the original state.

### EAA-025 — Irreversibility and retry/compensation boundary

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S044; DATE: 1993; WORK: Learning Interface Agents; VERSION: AAAI 1993, pp.459–465; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S043; DATE: 1997; WORK: Software Agents and User Autonomy; VERSION: Proceedings of Autonomous Agents 1997, pp.466–469; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S022; DATE: 1999; WORK: Validating the DS1 Remote Agent Experiment; VERSION: ISAIRAS 1999 primary experiment report; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Action-specific human control considering reversibility, combined analytically with acknowledgement ambiguity.

**Development:** Retain consequence-sensitive execution and recovery as analytical composition of the sources, not a newly claimed native transaction theory.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain consequence-sensitive execution and recovery as analytical composition of the sources, not a newly claimed native transaction theory.; OPERATION: Distinguish reversible, compensable, idempotent and irreversible actions where relevant; choose confirmation, evidence and recovery proportional to the actual effect, not just the command interface.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: A harmless idempotent action may be retried without additional ceremony when that property is established.

**Problem:** Retrying an unobserved action can duplicate an effect, while “undo” can be falsely treated as erasing harm.

**Mechanism:** Distinguish reversible, compensable, idempotent and irreversible actions where relevant; choose confirmation, evidence and recovery proportional to the actual effect, not just the command interface.

**Trigger and context:** External messages, resource spending, physical movement, destructive updates or uncertain action completion.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: Known effect semantics, credible completion evidence and authority for any compensating action.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Executor and authorising participant

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Executor and authorising participant; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Known effect semantics, credible completion evidence and authority for any compensating action.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Report material failure or unresolved effects; seek permission for genuine task changes and provide feasible handback. Consumer and prerequisite: Executor and authorising participant. Known effect semantics, credible completion evidence and authority for any compensating action.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** An uncertain irreversible effect does not trigger a blind duplicate; compensation is described as a new action with its own residual consequences.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A harmless idempotent action may be retried without additional ceremony when that property is established.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** The agent tradition does not supply a universal distributed-transaction guarantee; a user-approved action can still harm third parties.; Introduced cost or harm: Conservative delays, confirmation burden and incomplete restoration.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: The agent tradition does not supply a universal distributed-transaction guarantee; a user-approved action can still harm third parties.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C006; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S022; EAA-S043; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: The agent tradition does not supply a universal distributed-transaction guarantee; a user-approved action can still harm third parties.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S022; EAA-S043; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Idempotence and reversibility must be demonstrated for the operation and environment; neither follows from a button labelled undo.

**Unresolved questions:** What happens if this command already took effect, and can the proposed recovery actually restore what matters?; Idempotence and reversibility must be demonstrated for the operation and environment; neither follows from a button labelled undo.

### EAA-026 — Explicit time and resource envelope

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S013; DATE: 1955-02; WORK: A Behavioral Model of Rational Choice; VERSION: Quarterly Journal of Economics 69(1), 99–118; CMU archival scan; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S014; DATE: 1995-05; WORK: Provably Bounded-Optimal Agents; VERSION: JAIR 2, 575–609; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S035; DATE: 2024-07-01; WORK: AI Agents That Matter; VERSION: arXiv:2407.01502v1; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Aspiration/resource-relative reasoning and machine/environment-relative bounded optimality.

**Development:** Retain explicit whole-task constraints while comparing quality and costs separately instead of collapsing them into one misleading score.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain explicit whole-task constraints while comparing quality and costs separately instead of collapsing them into one misleading score.; OPERATION: Include deliberation, sensing, retries and coordination in the operative resource envelope; let remaining resources constrain action and continuation decisions.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: A simple fixed bound suffices for predictable bounded tasks; no optimiser is required merely to enforce a cap.

**Problem:** An apparently improving decision process can exceed the task’s time, compute, tool or attention budget.

**Mechanism:** Include deliberation, sensing, retries and coordination in the operative resource envelope; let remaining resources constrain action and continuation decisions.

**Trigger and context:** Finite deadlines, paid computation/tools, limited energy or human attention.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: Measurable or conservatively bounded costs, a decision horizon and an executable response to exhaustion.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Deliberation scheduler, delegator and evaluator

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Deliberation scheduler, delegator and evaluator; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Measurable or conservatively bounded costs, a decision horizon and an executable response to exhaustion.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Consultation consumes another actor’s time and may fail to receive a reply; attention is not free computation. Consumer and prerequisite: Deliberation scheduler, delegator and evaluator. Measurable or conservatively bounded costs, a decision horizon and an executable response to exhaustion.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** The agent does not treat reasoning or assistance as free, and can stop or fall back before exceeding the declared envelope.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A simple fixed bound suffices for predictable bounded tasks; no optimiser is required merely to enforce a cap.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Budget estimates may omit setup, human repair or retained infrastructure; a cheaper failed action may be worse overall.; Introduced cost or harm: Metering overhead and underuse of resources if limits are too conservative.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Budget estimates may omit setup, human repair or retained infrastructure; a cheaper failed action may be worse overall.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C003; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S035; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Budget estimates may omit setup, human repair or retained infrastructure; a cheaper failed action may be worse overall.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S035; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Bounded optimality is machine/environment relative; cost weights are task commitments, not universal constants.

**Unresolved questions:** Which resources can this task consume, including reasoning and repair, and what enforces the limit?; Bounded optimality is machine/environment relative; cost weights are task commitments, not universal constants.

**Property versus ceremony:** CEREMONY_ID: EAA-CE026; PROPERTY_ID: EAA-026; ARTEFACT_RITUAL_OR_BRAND: Aspiration/resource-relative reasoning and machine/environment-relative bounded optimality.; PROTECTED_PROPERTY_AND_FAILURE: An apparently improving decision process can exceed the task’s time, compute, tool or attention budget.; CONSUMER: Deliberation scheduler, delegator and evaluator; PREREQUISITES: Measurable or conservatively bounded costs, a decision horizon and an executable response to exhaustion.; MINIMAL_ADEQUATE_ALTERNATIVE: A simple fixed bound suffices for predictable bounded tasks; no optimiser is required merely to enforce a cap.; TRIGGER_FOR_FULLER_FORM: Finite deadlines, paid computation/tools, limited energy or human attention.; COSTS_OF_SIMPLIFICATION: Omitting this mechanism can reintroduce the specific failure: An apparently improving decision process can exceed the task’s time, compute, tool or attention budget. Simplification is acceptable only while the cheaper path still establishes the relevant criterion.; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT_CONDITIONS: Omit or retire a dedicated implementation when this trigger no longer applies, its consumer disappears, or an existing cheaper mechanism demonstrably establishes the same criterion. Do not erase relevant genealogy or unresolved effects. Specific non-trigger: A simple fixed bound suffices for predictable bounded tasks; no optimiser is required merely to enforce a cap.; CONTROL_COST: Metering overhead and underuse of resources if limits are too conservative.; ANTI_CEREMONY_BOUNDARY: The presence of an artefact, module, prompt or meeting is insufficient. The functional evidence is: The agent does not treat reasoning or assistance as free, and can stop or fall back before exceeding the declared envelope.

Earliest inspected anchor is not asserted to be the unique first origin.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S013; EAA-S014; EAA-S035; THEORETICAL_SUPPORT: STRENGTH: MODERATE_SCOPED; BASIS: The source states a formal result or model-relative mechanism. This study inspected the stated model/result and limitations, not an independent reconstruction of every proof; implementation and environmental adequacy remain separate.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S013; EAA-S014; COMPARATIVE_EVIDENCE: STRENGTH: MIXED_AND_SETTING_SPECIFIC; BASIS: Relevant studies or cases are recorded in the study-level evidence catalogue. ### EAA-027 — Anytime response or adequate deadline fallback

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S014; DATE: 1995-05; WORK: Provably Bounded-Optimal Agents; VERSION: JAIR 2, 575–609; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S046; DATE: 1995; WORK: Experiences with an Architecture for Intelligent, Reactive Agents; VERSION: ATAL 1995 workshop author copy; 17 pages; distinct later journal version; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Anytime/deadline-sensitive decision procedures and bounded reactive execution.

**Development:** Retain deadline adequacy, with anytime methods as one option rather than a universal architecture.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain deadline adequacy, with anytime methods as one option rather than a universal architecture.; OPERATION: Provide an adequate available decision or fallback by the relevant deadline; improve it while time remains only where the algorithm and execution context support interruption.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: A fast fixed policy can dominate an anytime solver; a batch answer is fine when no action deadline exists.

**Problem:** A solver may produce an excellent answer only after the decision opportunity has disappeared.

**Mechanism:** Provide an adequate available decision or fallback by the relevant deadline; improve it while time remains only where the algorithm and execution context support interruption.

**Trigger and context:** Hard or soft action deadlines and variable computation time.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A usable intermediate answer or fallback, an interruption mechanism and known timing obligations.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Runtime scheduler and executor

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Runtime scheduler and executor; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A usable intermediate answer or fallback, an interruption mechanism and known timing obligations.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Consultation consumes another actor’s time and may fail to receive a reply; attention is not free computation. Consumer and prerequisite: Runtime scheduler and executor. A usable intermediate answer or fallback, an interruption mechanism and known timing obligations.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** The system can produce or enact an adequate timely response rather than waiting indefinitely for an ideal result.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A fast fixed policy can dominate an anytime solver; a batch answer is fine when no action deadline exists.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Calling a module reactive or requesting a frequency does not make it hard real-time; some algorithms have no useful intermediate result.; Introduced cost or harm: Inferior early decisions and engineering overhead for interruptible computation.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Calling a module reactive or requesting a frequency does not make it hard real-time; some algorithms have no useful intermediate result.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C003; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S046; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Calling a module reactive or requesting a frequency does not make it hard real-time; some algorithms have no useful intermediate result.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S046; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Worst-case execution time and safe interruption may need separate evidence; simulated bounded optimality is not real-time certification.

**Unresolved questions:** What valid response exists when the available thinking time expires?; Worst-case execution time and safe interruption may need separate evidence; simulated bounded optimality is not real-time certification.

### EAA-028 — Metareasoning and value of computation

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S006; DATE: 1988; WORK: Plans and Resource-Bounded Practical Reasoning; VERSION: Computational Intelligence 4, 349–355; 30-page author-formatted manuscript; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S013; DATE: 1955-02; WORK: A Behavioral Model of Rational Choice; VERSION: Quarterly Journal of Economics 69(1), 99–118; CMU archival scan; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S014; DATE: 1995-05; WORK: Provably Bounded-Optimal Agents; VERSION: JAIR 2, 575–609; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Selection among feasible decision procedures based on computation quality and cost.

**Development:** Retain operational stopping/selection discipline; allow heuristics where a full metalevel calculation is unjustified.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain operational stopping/selection discipline; allow heuristics where a full metalevel calculation is unjustified.; OPERATION: Choose between further computation, acting, observing, consulting or terminating by an adequate estimate of expected improvement net of relevant delay and resource cost.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: A fixed cheap policy or simple stopping rule suffices where estimating the value of computation would cost more than it saves.

**Problem:** Reasoning about an action can improve it, but can also cost more than the improvement or make its premises stale.

**Mechanism:** Choose between further computation, acting, observing, consulting or terminating by an adequate estimate of expected improvement net of relevant delay and resource cost.

**Trigger and context:** Alternative reasoning procedures, variable difficulty or changing action windows.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: Available alternative actions/computations, interpretable costs and an estimate useful enough to influence selection.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Deliberation scheduler

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Deliberation scheduler; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Available alternative actions/computations, interpretable costs and an estimate useful enough to influence selection.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Consultation consumes another actor’s time and may fail to receive a reply; attention is not free computation. Consumer and prerequisite: Deliberation scheduler. Available alternative actions/computations, interpretable costs and an estimate useful enough to influence selection.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Further reasoning has a stated opportunity-cost justification rather than being invoked as a universal virtue.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A fixed cheap policy or simple stopping rule suffices where estimating the value of computation would cost more than it saves.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Metareasoning can recurse into another expensive optimisation; bounded-optimal results assume a specified machine and environment.; Introduced cost or harm: Metalevel computation and misestimated opportunity cost.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Metareasoning can recurse into another expensive optimisation; bounded-optimal results assume a specified machine and environment.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C003; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S015; EAA-S035; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Metareasoning can recurse into another expensive optimisation; bounded-optimal results assume a specified machine and environment.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S015; EAA-S035; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Expected decision improvement is uncertain and must not be mistaken for guaranteed benefit.

**Unresolved questions:** What evidence makes another unit of reasoning better than the best currently available action?; Expected decision improvement is uncertain and must not be mistaken for guaranteed benefit.
