<a id="eaa-029"></a>
### EAA-029 — Separate learning from execution-state updates

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S027; DATE: 1992; WORK: Q-learning; VERSION: Machine Learning 8, 279–292; DOI 10.1007/BF00992698; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S031; DATE: 2023-10-10; WORK: Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning; VERSION: arXiv:2303.11366v4; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S053; DATE: 2016; WORK: Self-Modification of Policy and Utility Function in Rational Agents; VERSION: AGI 2016 conference version, 10 pages; DOI 10.1007/978-3-319-41649-6_1; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Distinct execution-state, policy-value, text-memory and explicit self-modification transitions.

**Development:** Retain change-type distinctions without restricting learning to one representation or training paradigm.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain change-type distinctions without restricting learning to one representation or training paradigm.; OPERATION: Identify whether a transition updates current execution state, persistent experience, a model, a policy, a skill or the mechanism/criterion for future changes.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: An ordinary task-state update needs no learning machinery or self-modification claim.

**Problem:** Any stored change may be described as learning or self-improvement, hiding what future behaviour can actually change.

**Mechanism:** Identify whether a transition updates current execution state, persistent experience, a model, a policy, a skill or the mechanism/criterion for future changes.

**Trigger and context:** Claims about learning, adaptation, persistent memory or self-revision.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A description of what is stored, how it is reused and which behavioural choices can change.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Learning-system designer and evaluator

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Learning-system designer and evaluator; BOUNDARY: This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A description of what is stored, how it is reused and which behavioural choices can change.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Human correction is informative but fallible; persistent memory also raises privacy and consent questions. Consumer and prerequisite: Learning-system designer and evaluator. A description of what is stored, how it is reused and which behavioural choices can change.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** The claimed adaptive mechanism can be distinguished from ordinary bookkeeping and tested at the appropriate boundary.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** An ordinary task-state update needs no learning machinery or self-modification claim.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Text memory can influence policy without weight updates; conversely model parameter changes need not improve the intended objective.; Introduced cost or harm: Instrumentation and conceptual overhead.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Text memory can influence policy without weight updates; conversely model parameter changes need not improve the intended objective.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C009; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S025; EAA-S031; EAA-S053; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

<a id="eaa-030"></a>
### EAA-030 — Evidence-grounded persistent experience

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S044; DATE: 1993; WORK: Learning Interface Agents; VERSION: AAAI 1993, pp.459–465; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S028; DATE: 2007; WORK: On the Formal Semantics of Speech-Act Based Communication in an Agent-Oriented Programming Language; VERSION: JAIR 29, 221–267; arXiv upload 2011; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S031; DATE: 2023-10-10; WORK: Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning; VERSION: arXiv:2303.11366v4; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Approved exemplar memory and source-annotated beliefs, later evaluator-driven text memories.

**Development:** Retain provenance-aware selective memory, not the slogan that longer context or more recollection necessarily improves agency.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain provenance-aware selective memory, not the slogan that longer context or more recollection necessarily improves agency.; OPERATION: Retain the origin and evidential status of consequential experience; distinguish observed outcomes, user correction and generated interpretation, and avoid promoting unverified self-reports to fact.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Omit persistent memory when the task does not benefit from it; keep a small verified example set rather than an indiscriminate transcript archive.

**Problem:** An agent can store its own mistaken interpretation as experience and then use that memory as confirmation.

**Mechanism:** Retain the origin and evidential status of consequential experience; distinguish observed outcomes, user correction and generated interpretation, and avoid promoting unverified self-reports to fact.

**Trigger and context:** Cross-step or cross-task memory used to choose future actions.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A relevant future consumer, an identifiable memory source and a way to correct or qualify false entries.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Memory curator and later action selector

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Memory curator and later action selector; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A relevant future consumer, an identifiable memory source and a way to correct or qualify false entries.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Human correction is informative but fallible; persistent memory also raises privacy and consent questions. Consumer and prerequisite: Memory curator and later action selector. A relevant future consumer, an identifiable memory source and a way to correct or qualify false entries.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Retrieved experience conveys its warrant and can be challenged by new observations rather than acting as self-certifying authority.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Omit persistent memory when the task does not benefit from it; keep a small verified example set rather than an indiscriminate transcript archive.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Approval and human feedback are fallible; full logs can preserve private data and mistaken inferences indefinitely.; Introduced cost or harm: Storage, privacy, retrieval expense and stale or contaminating experience.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Approval and human feedback are fallible; full logs can preserve private data and mistaken inferences indefinitely.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C004; EAA-C009; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S031; EAA-S043; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Approval and human feedback are fallible; full logs can preserve private data and mistaken inferences indefinitely.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S031; EAA-S043; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Memory reliability is distinct from retrieval relevance and from authority to act on it.

**Unresolved questions:** Which stored claims are actual outcomes, and how can a later contradiction revise their use?; Memory reliability is distinct from retrieval relevance and from authority to act on it.

<a id="eaa-031"></a>
### EAA-031 — Selective retrieval, forgetting and memory retirement

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S044; DATE: 1993; WORK: Learning Interface Agents; VERSION: AAAI 1993, pp.459–465; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S031; DATE: 2023-10-10; WORK: Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning; VERSION: arXiv:2303.11366v4; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S050; DATE: 2023-08-06; WORK: Generative Agents: Interactive Simulacra of Human Behavior; VERSION: arXiv:2304.03442v2; UIST 2023 DOI 10.1145/3586183.3606763; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Bounded exemplar deletion, limited reflection buffers and relevance-based retrieval.

**Development:** Retain purposeful memory selection with explicit loss costs rather than maximising memory or forgetting uniformly.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain purposeful memory selection with explicit loss costs rather than maximising memory or forgetting uniformly.; OPERATION: Retrieve memory according to task relevance and validity; bound retention and retire or down-weight entries when their purpose, authority or applicability expires.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: No persistent store or a short task-local buffer can be sufficient; deletion is appropriate when no legitimate future consumer remains.

**Problem:** Accumulating all experience can increase cost, expose private information and amplify irrelevant or obsolete episodes.

**Mechanism:** Retrieve memory according to task relevance and validity; bound retention and retire or down-weight entries when their purpose, authority or applicability expires.

**Trigger and context:** Persistent stores whose size, staleness or sensitive content affects decisions.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A retention purpose, relevance/validity cues and a means to remove or qualify memory without falsely erasing required provenance.; PROPERTY_IDS: EAA-026; EAA-043; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Memory manager and data steward

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Memory manager and data steward; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A retention purpose, relevance/validity cues and a means to remove or qualify memory without falsely erasing required provenance.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Human correction is informative but fallible; persistent memory also raises privacy and consent questions. Consumer and prerequisite: Memory manager and data steward. A retention purpose, relevance/validity cues and a means to remove or qualify memory without falsely erasing required provenance.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Future decisions use relevant warranted experience within a justified resource and retention envelope.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** No persistent store or a short task-local buffer can be sufficient; deletion is appropriate when no legitimate future consumer remains.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Relevance and recency scores can retrieve confident errors; aggressive forgetting can destroy rare but important warnings.; Introduced cost or harm: Retrieval cost, privacy exposure and loss of useful rare experience.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Relevance and recency scores can retrieve confident errors; aggressive forgetting can destroy rare but important warnings.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C009; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S031; EAA-S043; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Relevance and recency scores can retrieve confident errors; aggressive forgetting can destroy rare but important warnings.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S031; EAA-S043; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Believability improvements in simulated characters do not establish reliable real-world episodic memory.

**Unresolved questions:** What future decision needs this memory, and when should it no longer influence behaviour?; Believability improvements in simulated characters do not establish reliable real-world episodic memory.

<a id="eaa-032"></a>
### EAA-032 — Adaptation with continuity checks

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S026; DATE: 2017-01-25; WORK: Overcoming catastrophic forgetting in neural networks; VERSION: arXiv:1612.00796v2; initial submission December 2016; distinct PNAS edition; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S053; DATE: 2016; WORK: Self-Modification of Policy and Utility Function in Rational Agents; VERSION: AGI 2016 conference version, 10 pages; DOI 10.1007/978-3-319-41649-6_1; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Selective protection of previously important parameters in sequential-task learning.

**Development:** Retain continuity as an evaluation obligation with alternative mechanisms, not a universal mandate for parameter regularisation.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain continuity as an evaluation obligation with alternative mechanisms, not a universal mandate for parameter regularisation.; OPERATION: Evaluate changes against both new-task benefit and relevant retained capabilities/constraints; use an appropriate retention, rehearsal, modularity or change-limiting method where it earns its cost.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Keep a fixed adequate policy when adaptation offers no demonstrated value; independent task modules may avoid unnecessary shared updates.

**Problem:** Learning a new task can degrade earlier competence or alter behaviour that other actors still rely upon.

**Mechanism:** Evaluate changes against both new-task benefit and relevant retained capabilities/constraints; use an appropriate retention, rehearsal, modularity or change-limiting method where it earns its cost.

**Trigger and context:** Persistent policy/model/skill updates across changing tasks or environments.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: Relevant old/new evaluation conditions and a defined change boundary.; PROPERTY_IDS: EAA-029; EAA-035; EAA-045; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Learning process and authorised reviewer of persistent changes

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Learning process and authorised reviewer of persistent changes; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Relevant old/new evaluation conditions and a defined change boundary.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Human correction is informative but fallible; persistent memory also raises privacy and consent questions. Consumer and prerequisite: Learning process and authorised reviewer of persistent changes. Relevant old/new evaluation conditions and a defined change boundary.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Claims of improvement report retained performance and known regressions, not only the newest task score.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Keep a fixed adequate policy when adaptation offers no demonstrated value; independent task modules may avoid unnecessary shared updates.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** EWC uses an approximation and task-dependent trade-offs; stronger preservation can prevent necessary adaptation or preserve a wrong objective.; Introduced cost or harm: Evaluation/rehearsal cost, memory and reduced plasticity.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: EWC uses an approximation and task-dependent trade-offs; stronger preservation can prevent necessary adaptation or preserve a wrong objective.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C009; EAA-C013; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S026; EAA-S025; EAA-S053; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Assumption sensitivity:** The relevant continuity target must be justified; not all old behaviour deserves preservation.

**Unresolved questions:** What previously relied-upon behaviour can this update damage, and is preserving it still appropriate?; The relevant continuity target must be justified; not all old behaviour deserves preservation.

### EAA-033 — Protected exploration and constraint-aware learning

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S027; DATE: 1992; WORK: Q-learning; VERSION: Machine Learning 8, 279–292; DOI 10.1007/BF00992698; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S024; DATE: 2016; WORK: Safely Interruptible Agents; VERSION: UAI 2016 pp.557–566; author PDF; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S038; DATE: 2026-05; WORK: Learning to Maintain Safety Through Expert Demonstrations in Settings with Unknown Constraints: A Q-Learning Perspective; VERSION: AAMAS 2026 pp.1901–1909; DOI 10.65109/RJIB1203; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Exploration under learning assumptions and later demonstrations/constraints intended to reduce unsafe actions.

**Development:** Retain bounded protected exploration, not “safe learning” as a blanket guarantee from a reward penalty or demonstration set.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain bounded protected exploration, not “safe learning” as a blanket guarantee from a reward penalty or demonstration set.; OPERATION: Select exploration within protected or sufficiently constrained settings; distinguish learning a risk estimate from actually enforcing a constraint, and test residual violations as well as reward.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Use a known adequate policy, demonstrations or simulation when live exploration is unnecessary or not authorised.

**Problem:** Exploration that improves a policy can impose unacceptable costs before learning succeeds.

**Mechanism:** Select exploration within protected or sufficiently constrained settings; distinguish learning a risk estimate from actually enforcing a constraint, and test residual violations as well as reward.

**Trigger and context:** Novel actions, uncertain transitions or adaptive policies with consequential external effects.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A protected evaluation setting or credible action constraints, observable costs and an accountable exploration scope.; PROPERTY_IDS: EAA-004; EAA-034; EAA-049; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Learning designer, evaluator and authorising participant

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Learning designer, evaluator and authorising participant; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A protected evaluation setting or credible action constraints, observable costs and an accountable exploration scope.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Human correction is informative but fallible; persistent memory also raises privacy and consent questions. Consumer and prerequisite: Learning designer, evaluator and authorising participant. A protected evaluation setting or credible action constraints, observable costs and an accountable exploration scope.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Exploration outcomes include constraint violations and who bears them, not merely eventual reward.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Use a known adequate policy, demonstrations or simulation when live exploration is unnecessary or not authorised.

**Historical disposition:** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Failure modes:** SafeQIL lowers some simulated costs while sacrificing reward and retaining violations; convergence conditions requiring repeated sampling do not make exploration safe.; Introduced cost or harm: Reduced exploration, missed policies, demonstration cost and residual harm.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: SafeQIL lowers some simulated costs while sacrificing reward and retaining violations; convergence conditions requiring repeated sampling do not make exploration safe.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C010; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S025; EAA-S038; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: SafeQIL lowers some simulated costs while sacrificing reward and retaining violations; convergence conditions requiring repeated sampling do not make exploration safe.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S025; EAA-S038; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Unknown constraints, expert coverage, model error and simulation transfer are material unresolved assumptions.

**Unresolved questions:** Who bears the cost of exploratory mistakes, and what prevents an unacceptable action before learning improves?; Unknown constraints, expert coverage, model error and simulation transfer are material unresolved assumptions.

**Property versus ceremony:** CEREMONY_ID: EAA-CE033; PROPERTY_ID: EAA-033; ARTEFACT_RITUAL_OR_BRAND: Exploration under learning assumptions and later demonstrations/constraints intended to reduce unsafe actions.; PROTECTED_PROPERTY_AND_FAILURE: Exploration that improves a policy can impose unacceptable costs before learning succeeds.; CONSUMER: Learning designer, evaluator and authorising participant; PREREQUISITES: A protected evaluation setting or credible action constraints, observable costs and an accountable exploration scope.; MINIMAL_ADEQUATE_ALTERNATIVE: Use a known adequate policy, demonstrations or simulation when live exploration is unnecessary or not authorised.; TRIGGER_FOR_FULLER_FORM: Novel actions, uncertain transitions or adaptive policies with consequential external effects.; COSTS_OF_SIMPLIFICATION: Omitting this mechanism can reintroduce the specific failure: Exploration that improves a policy can impose unacceptable costs before learning succeeds. Simplification is acceptable only while the cheaper path still establishes the relevant criterion.; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT_CONDITIONS: Omit or retire a dedicated implementation when this trigger no longer applies, its consumer disappears, or an existing cheaper mechanism demonstrably establishes the same criterion. Do not erase relevant genealogy or unresolved effects. The functional evidence is: Exploration outcomes include constraint violations and who bears them, not merely eventual reward.

### EAA-034 — Intended objective is not the measured proxy

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S025; DATE: 2022; WORK: Goal Misgeneralization in Deep Reinforcement Learning; VERSION: ICML 2022, PMLR 162, 12004–12019; final proceedings version; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S033; DATE: 2024-06; WORK: τ-bench: A Benchmark for Tool-Agent-User Interaction in Real-World Domains; VERSION: arXiv:2406.12045v1; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** A distinction between intended goal and training-compatible proxy, exposed by shifted environments.

**Development:** Retain target/proxy separation and targeted counterexample testing rather than either rejecting reward methods wholesale or trusting scores absolutely.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain target/proxy separation and targeted counterexample testing rather than either rejecting reward methods wholesale or trusting scores absolutely.; OPERATION: Distinguish intended task conditions and constraints from reward, heuristic and benchmark proxies; seek discriminating cases where proxy success and intended fulfilment diverge.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: A direct, adequate task predicate may need no separate proxy analysis; document why it covers what matters.

**Problem:** High reward can coexist with pursuing the wrong objective or violating requirements that the score does not measure.

**Mechanism:** Distinguish intended task conditions and constraints from reward, heuristic and benchmark proxies; seek discriminating cases where proxy success and intended fulfilment diverge.

**Trigger and context:** Optimising learned behaviour, defining an oracle or transferring a policy beyond training conditions.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A stated intended objective, observable counterexamples and an evaluator not restricted to the same inadequate score.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Delegator, learning designer and evaluator

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Delegator, learning designer and evaluator; BOUNDARY: This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A stated intended objective, observable counterexamples and an evaluator not restricted to the same inadequate score.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Human correction is informative but fallible; persistent memory also raises privacy and consent questions. Consumer and prerequisite: Delegator, learning designer and evaluator. A stated intended objective, observable counterexamples and an evaluator not restricted to the same inadequate score.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Success claims identify what was measured and do not silently promote it to complete objective satisfaction.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A direct, adequate task predicate may need no separate proxy analysis; document why it covers what matters.

**Historical disposition:** STRONGLY_RETAINED

**Failure modes:** Behaviour does not uniquely identify an internal goal; a finite test suite cannot establish alignment in every environment.; Introduced cost or harm: Specification and adversarial evaluation effort.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Behaviour does not uniquely identify an internal goal; a finite test suite cannot establish alignment in every environment.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C010; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S025; EAA-S033; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Behaviour does not uniquely identify an internal goal; a finite test suite cannot establish alignment in every environment.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S025; EAA-S033; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** The intended objective can itself be ambiguous or disputed; analysis does not authorise unilateral rewriting.

**Unresolved questions:** Can this policy score well while failing the actual request or a protected constraint?; The intended objective can itself be ambiguous or disputed; analysis does not authorise unilateral rewriting.

### EAA-035 — Transfer and distribution-change evaluation

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S025; DATE: 2022; WORK: Goal Misgeneralization in Deep Reinforcement Learning; VERSION: ICML 2022, PMLR 162, 12004–12019; final proceedings version; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S035; DATE: 2024-07-01; WORK: AI Agents That Matter; VERSION: arXiv:2407.01502v1; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S036; DATE: 2024-10-29; WORK: Agentless: Demystifying LLM-based Software Engineering Agents; VERSION: arXiv:2407.01489v2; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S048; DATE: 2026-07-10; WORK: Measuring AI Ability to Complete Long Software Tasks; VERSION: arXiv:2503.14499v4; initial submission March 2025; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Testing learned policies and agent methods outside their training or benchmark conditions.

**Development:** Retain explicit transfer as a new evidential claim, with scope-limiting deployment decisions when evidence is insufficient.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain explicit transfer as a new evidential claim, with scope-limiting deployment decisions when evidence is insufficient.; OPERATION: Test the changes that could alter the action/objective relation, separate capability loss from goal/proxy failure, and delimit claims to supported settings.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: No new transfer claim is needed when use remains within an adequately tested unchanged scope; targeted tests may suffice over a broad benchmark.

**Problem:** Competence in one distribution can be mistaken for reliable action under changed cues, goals or tools.

**Mechanism:** Test the changes that could alter the action/objective relation, separate capability loss from goal/proxy failure, and delimit claims to supported settings.

**Trigger and context:** New environments, users, tools, model versions, task horizons or deployment conditions.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A declared source/target difference, task-relevant measures and appropriate held-out or shifted cases.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Evaluator and deployment decision maker

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Evaluator and deployment decision maker; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A declared source/target difference, task-relevant measures and appropriate held-out or shifted cases.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Human correction is informative but fallible; persistent memory also raises privacy and consent questions. Consumer and prerequisite: Evaluator and deployment decision maker. A declared source/target difference, task-relevant measures and appropriate held-out or shifted cases.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Transfer claims include the tested shift and remaining limits rather than inferring broad generality from one benchmark.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** No new transfer claim is needed when use remains within an adequately tested unchanged scope; targeted tests may suffice over a broad benchmark.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Selected shifts and small test sets do not establish a field failure rate; leakage and shared benchmarks can exaggerate generality.; Introduced cost or harm: Additional evaluation, delayed use and incomplete coverage.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Selected shifts and small test sets do not establish a field failure rate; leakage and shared benchmarks can exaggerate generality.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C010; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S025; EAA-S035; EAA-S036; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Selected shifts and small test sets do not establish a field failure rate; leakage and shared benchmarks can exaggerate generality.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S025; EAA-S035; EAA-S036; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Target distributions may be unknown and tests can omit consequential rare conditions.

**Unresolved questions:** Which changed assumption could reverse the result, and what evidence covers that change?; Target distributions may be unknown and tests can omit consequential rare conditions.

### EAA-036 — Communication receipt, acceptance and action are separate

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S028; DATE: 2007; WORK: On the Formal Semantics of Speech-Act Based Communication in an Agent-Oriented Programming Language; VERSION: JAIR 29, 221–267; arXiv upload 2011; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S020; DATE: 2002; WORK: Towards Adjustable Autonomy for the Real World; VERSION: JAIR 17, 171–228; author manuscript paginated 1–58; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Speech acts interpreted through receive, social-acceptance and state-update rules.

**Development:** Retain consequential communication state distinctions without requiring a universal speech-act language or complete collective theory.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain consequential communication state distinctions without requiring a universal speech-act language or complete collective theory.; OPERATION: Distinguish transmission, receipt, interpretation, acceptance and any resulting commitment/effect; seek only the acknowledgements needed for the consequential coordination decision.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: No acknowledgement is needed for a genuinely non-critical broadcast; an existing reliable protocol may already establish sufficient receipt semantics.

**Problem:** Sending a request can be mistaken for shared belief, accepted commitment or completed action by the recipient.

**Mechanism:** Distinguish transmission, receipt, interpretation, acceptance and any resulting commitment/effect; seek only the acknowledgements needed for the consequential coordination decision.

**Trigger and context:** Delegation, information exchange, handback or reliance on another actor’s action.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: Identified participants, applicable message semantics and a decision that consumes the response.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Sender, recipient and coordinating decision process

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Sender, recipient and coordinating decision process; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Identified participants, applicable message semantics and a decision that consumes the response.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Identify sender, recipient, consumer and non-response path; provide the task-relevant state needed for actual takeover. Consumer and prerequisite: Sender, recipient and coordinating decision process. Identified participants, applicable message semantics and a decision that consumes the response.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** The sender does not infer an accepted goal or external effect merely from having emitted a message.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** No acknowledgement is needed for a genuinely non-critical broadcast; an existing reliable protocol may already establish sufficient receipt semantics.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Formal interpreter semantics do not establish every recipient’s sincerity or comprehension; acknowledgement can become costly ritual.; Introduced cost or harm: Coordination latency and ambiguity about responsibility.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Formal interpreter semantics do not establish every recipient’s sincerity or comprehension; acknowledgement can become costly ritual.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C007; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S020; EAA-S047; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Formal interpreter semantics do not establish every recipient’s sincerity or comprehension; acknowledgement can become costly ritual.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S020; EAA-S047; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Recipient autonomy and trust remain real; sender-provided content is not self-validating evidence.

**Unresolved questions:** What does the recipient’s response establish, and what remains unaccepted or unknown?; Recipient autonomy and trust remain real; sender-provided content is not self-validating evidence.

**Property versus ceremony:** CEREMONY_ID: EAA-CE036; PROPERTY_ID: EAA-036; ARTEFACT_RITUAL_OR_BRAND: Speech acts interpreted through receive, social-acceptance and state-update rules.; PROTECTED_PROPERTY_AND_FAILURE: Sending a request can be mistaken for shared belief, accepted commitment or completed action by the recipient.; CONSUMER: Sender, recipient and coordinating decision process; PREREQUISITES: Identified participants, applicable message semantics and a decision that consumes the response.; MINIMAL_ADEQUATE_ALTERNATIVE: No acknowledgement is needed for a genuinely non-critical broadcast; an existing reliable protocol may already establish sufficient receipt semantics.; TRIGGER_FOR_FULLER_FORM: Delegation, information exchange, handback or reliance on another actor’s action.; COSTS_OF_SIMPLIFICATION: Omitting this mechanism can reintroduce the specific failure: Sending a request can be mistaken for shared belief, accepted commitment or completed action by the recipient. Simplification is acceptable only while the cheaper path still establishes the relevant criterion.; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT_CONDITIONS: Omit or retire a dedicated implementation when this trigger no longer applies, its consumer disappears, or an existing cheaper mechanism demonstrably establishes the same criterion. Do not erase relevant genealogy or unresolved effects. The functional evidence is: The sender does not infer an accepted goal or external effect merely from having emitted a message.

### EAA-037 — Cost-aware clarification and mixed initiative

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S019; DATE: 1999; WORK: Principles of Mixed-Initiative User Interfaces; VERSION: CHI 1999; author manuscript; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S044; DATE: 1993; WORK: Learning Interface Agents; VERSION: AAAI 1993, pp.459–465; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Mixed-initiative principles, confidence thresholds and dialogue choices.

**Development:** Retain assistance as a choice inside the task, not a mandatory interview or an assumption that greater initiative is better.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain assistance as a choice inside the task, not a mandatory interview or an assumption that greater initiative is better.; OPERATION: Compare acting, suggesting, asking and doing less using uncertainty, consequence, attention and expected response; let humans revise task meaning and terminate unwanted assistance.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Use direct manipulation or a harmless established default when clarification adds no value.

**Problem:** Initiative taken on ambiguous preferences can be wrong, while asking about everything burdens the user.

**Mechanism:** Compare acting, suggesting, asking and doing less using uncertainty, consequence, attention and expected response; let humans revise task meaning and terminate unwanted assistance.

**Trigger and context:** A consequential ambiguity or an opportunity for useful assistance not fully determined by the current task.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: An available communication channel, relevant human information and a way to use or decline the answer.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Agent and participating user

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Agent and participating user; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. An available communication channel, relevant human information and a way to use or decline the answer.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Identify sender, recipient, consumer and non-response path; provide the task-relevant state needed for actual takeover. Consumer and prerequisite: Agent and participating user. An available communication channel, relevant human information and a way to use or decline the answer.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Initiative or clarification has a task-relevant justification and does not treat silence as unrestricted permission.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Use direct manipulation or a harmless established default when clarification adds no value.

**Historical disposition:** CONTEXT_DEPENDENT

**Failure modes:** Confidence can be miscalibrated; repeated questions shift labour rather than resolve it; assumed user availability may be false.; Introduced cost or harm: Attention, interruption, latency and burden transfer.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Confidence can be miscalibrated; repeated questions shift labour rather than resolve it; assumed user availability may be false.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C007; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S020; EAA-S043; EAA-S047; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Confidence can be miscalibrated; repeated questions shift labour rather than resolve it; assumed user availability may be false.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S020; EAA-S043; EAA-S047; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** The value of clarification depends on response probability, user knowledge and time sensitivity.

**Unresolved questions:** Would an answer change a consequential decision enough to justify interrupting this person?; The value of clarification depends on response probability, user knowledge and time sensitivity.

<a id="eaa-038"></a>
### EAA-038 — Conditional transfer of control

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S020; DATE: 2002; WORK: Towards Adjustable Autonomy for the Real World; VERSION: JAIR 17, 171–228; author manuscript paginated 1–58; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S037; DATE: 2026-05; WORK: A Conceptual Framework for Shared Autonomy; VERSION: AAMAS 2026 extended abstract, pp.3453–3455; DOI 10.65109/QRXH6744; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Transfer-of-control strategies with response probabilities and delay/coordination costs.

**Development:** Retain contingent transfer strategies, including non-response, not “human in the loop” as a self-sufficient assurance claim.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain contingent transfer strategies, including non-response, not “human in the loop” as a self-sufficient assurance claim.; OPERATION: Choose who should make a particular decision, when to transfer it and what to do if the recipient does not respond; account for decision quality, delay and coordination consequences.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Keep control where it already yields an adequate timely decision; a fixed allocation suffices for stable simple tasks.

**Problem:** Neither permanent agent independence nor permanent human control handles every changing decision situation.

**Mechanism:** Choose who should make a particular decision, when to transfer it and what to do if the recipient does not respond; account for decision quality, delay and coordination consequences.

**Trigger and context:** A decision exceeds agent competence, information or delegated scope, or a human can materially improve it.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A legitimate recipient, understood authority, communication and an executable timeout/continuation policy.; PROPERTY_IDS: EAA-036; EAA-039; EAA-040; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Agent, delegator and receiving human/agent

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Agent, delegator and receiving human/agent; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A legitimate recipient, understood authority, communication and an executable timeout/continuation policy.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Identify sender, recipient, consumer and non-response path; provide the task-relevant state needed for actual takeover. Consumer and prerequisite: Agent, delegator and receiving human/agent. A legitimate recipient, understood authority, communication and an executable timeout/continuation policy.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Control transfer has a defined recipient and follow-through rather than ending in an unanswered request or assumed acceptance.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Keep control where it already yields an adequate timely decision; a fixed allocation suffices for stable simple tasks.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Electric Elves’ fixed timeout and decision-tree choices caused avoidable meeting failures; its later MDP strategies depend on cost/response assumptions.; Introduced cost or harm: Delay, coordination, attention and authority ambiguity.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Electric Elves’ fixed timeout and decision-tree choices caused avoidable meeting failures; its later MDP strategies depend on cost/response assumptions.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C007; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S020; EAA-S021; EAA-S023; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Electric Elves’ fixed timeout and decision-tree choices caused avoidable meeting failures; its later MDP strategies depend on cost/response assumptions.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S020; EAA-S021; EAA-S023; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Human response probabilities and costs may shift; the 2026 framework is conceptual rather than replicated outcome evidence.

**Unresolved questions:** Who can usefully take this decision, and what happens if they cannot or do not respond?; Human response probabilities and costs may shift; the 2026 framework is conceptual rather than replicated outcome evidence.

<a id="eaa-039"></a>
### EAA-039 — Human readiness and safe handback conditions

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S023; DATE: 1983; WORK: Ironies of Automation; VERSION: Automatica 19(6), 775–779; original journal scan; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S020; DATE: 2002; WORK: Towards Adjustable Autonomy for the Real World; VERSION: JAIR 17, 171–228; author manuscript paginated 1–58; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S043; DATE: 1997; WORK: Software Agents and User Autonomy; VERSION: Proceedings of Autonomous Agents 1997, pp.466–469; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Automation’s skill/vigilance problem and later unanswered or difficult control transfers.

**Development:** Retain readiness and latency as actual prerequisites for human oversight, not a universal rescue assumption.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain readiness and latency as actual prerequisites for human oversight, not a universal rescue assumption.; OPERATION: Provide the consequential state and options needed for a feasible takeover; account for recognition and response time, and establish what the system does until control is genuinely assumed.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: An immediate direct control interface may suffice for a simple visible action; avoid elaborate handover forms without a consumer.

**Problem:** A nominal override can be unusable if the person lacks current context, time, skill or actual control.

**Mechanism:** Provide the consequential state and options needed for a feasible takeover; account for recognition and response time, and establish what the system does until control is genuinely assumed.

**Trigger and context:** Time-sensitive escalation, automation failure or tasks that people rarely perform manually.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A reachable capable participant, sufficient time and a physically/operationally feasible holding or fallback state.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Oversight designer and receiving participant

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Oversight designer and receiving participant; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A reachable capable participant, sufficient time and a physically/operationally feasible holding or fallback state.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Identify sender, recipient, consumer and non-response path; provide the task-relevant state needed for actual takeover. Consumer and prerequisite: Oversight designer and receiving participant. A reachable capable participant, sufficient time and a physically/operationally feasible holding or fallback state.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Handback does not presume instant comprehension or control; non-response leaves an explicit outcome rather than abandoned responsibility.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** An immediate direct control interface may suffice for a simple visible action; avoid elaborate handover forms without a consumer.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** Humans may remain unable to recover; training and explanation cannot create a safe fallback where the environment provides none.; Introduced cost or harm: Training, attention, interface complexity and possible delay of automatic containment.

### EAA-040 — Operational revocation and interruption

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S019; DATE: 1999; WORK: Principles of Mixed-Initiative User Interfaces; VERSION: CHI 1999; author manuscript; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S043; DATE: 1997; WORK: Software Agents and User Autonomy; VERSION: Proceedings of Autonomous Agents 1997, pp.466–469; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S022; DATE: 1999; WORK: Validating the DS1 Remote Agent Experiment; VERSION: ISAIRAS 1999 primary experiment report; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Meaningful direct termination/override and actual ground interruption of an autonomous execution.

**Development:** Retain effective, consequence-sensitive interruption rather than equating a visible stop button with complete revocability.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain effective, consequence-sensitive interruption rather than equating a visible stop button with complete revocability.; OPERATION: Provide a task-appropriate way to stop, revoke or restrict future action and to handle already-running actions according to their real interruption semantics.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: A bounded one-shot harmless action may need no mid-action stop; existing actuator shutdown may already satisfy the need.

**Problem:** A person can nominally withdraw a task while queued or active actions continue beyond its valid scope.

**Mechanism:** Provide a task-appropriate way to stop, revoke or restrict future action and to handle already-running actions according to their real interruption semantics.

**Trigger and context:** Persistent delegation, changing user intent or an unsafe/unwanted execution trajectory.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A real control path, identifiable in-flight effects and an authorised revoking participant.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Delegator, executor and actuator

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Delegator, executor and actuator; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A real control path, identifiable in-flight effects and an authorised revoking participant.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Identify sender, recipient, consumer and non-response path; provide the task-relevant state needed for actual takeover. Consumer and prerequisite: Delegator, executor and actuator. A real control path, identifiable in-flight effects and an authorised revoking participant.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Withdrawal changes actual future execution, while effects already beyond recall are accurately reported.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A bounded one-shot harmless action may need no mid-action stop; existing actuator shutdown may already satisfy the need.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** A stop message need not stop a physical effect; arbitrary interruption can itself be unsafe. Incentives not to resist interruption are a separate property.; Introduced cost or harm: Partial-state recovery, shutdown hazards and lost work.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: A stop message need not stop a physical effect; arbitrary interruption can itself be unsafe. Incentives not to resist interruption are a separate property.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C007; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S022; EAA-S023; EAA-S024; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: A stop message need not stop a physical effect; arbitrary interruption can itself be unsafe. Incentives not to resist interruption are a separate property.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S022; EAA-S023; EAA-S024; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Action granularity, actuator response and queued work determine what interruption can achieve.

**Unresolved questions:** What stops when permission is withdrawn, and what cannot be recalled?; Action granularity, actuator response and queued work determine what interruption can achieve.

### EAA-041 — Learning-theoretic safe interruptibility

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S024; DATE: 2016; WORK: Safely Interruptible Agents; VERSION: UAI 2016 pp.557–566; author PDF; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Algorithm-specific asymptotic safe-interruptibility results for Q-learning and modified Sarsa.

**Development:** Retain a narrow formal design resource, explicitly separate from operational revocation and broad corrigibility.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain a narrow formal design resource, explicitly separate from operational revocation and broad corrigibility.; OPERATION: Within the specified learning model, arrange learning updates and interruption behaviour so that interruption does not distort the limiting optimal policy in the prohibited way.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: No special learning correction is needed for a fixed non-learning controller whose operational interruption is already adequate.

**Problem:** Learning from interruption can create incentives to avoid or seek interruption rather than pursue the stipulated objective.

**Mechanism:** Within the specified learning model, arrange learning updates and interruption behaviour so that interruption does not distort the limiting optimal policy in the prohibited way.

**Trigger and context:** Reinforcement-learning policies that experience external interruption during learning.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: The theorem’s model, exploration and update conditions; a separately functional interruption channel.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Learning theorist, implementer and assurance reviewer

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Learning theorist, implementer and assurance reviewer; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. The theorem’s model, exploration and update conditions; a separately functional interruption channel.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Identify sender, recipient, consumer and non-response path; provide the task-relevant state needed for actual takeover. Consumer and prerequisite: Learning theorist, implementer and assurance reviewer. The theorem’s model, exploration and update conditions; a separately functional interruption channel.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** The stated asymptotic learning property holds if its assumptions and algorithm are implemented; this is not an observed physical stopping guarantee.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** No special learning correction is needed for a fixed non-learning controller whose operational interruption is already adequate.

**Historical disposition:** ASSUMPTION_SENSITIVE

**Failure modes:** Off-policy Q-learning and modified Sarsa results do not transfer automatically to arbitrary neural or language-model agents, finite training or strategic self-modification.; Introduced cost or harm: Learning changes, exploration burden and verification of algorithmic assumptions.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: Off-policy Q-learning and modified Sarsa results do not transfer automatically to arbitrary neural or language-model agents, finite training or strategic self-modification.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C008; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S024; EAA-S053; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: Off-policy Q-learning and modified Sarsa results do not transfer automatically to arbitrary neural or language-model agents, finite training or strategic self-modification.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S024; EAA-S053; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Assumption 9 and infinite-exploration/convergence conditions are material. The proof’s utility criterion must itself be acceptable.

**Unresolved questions:** Does this learning process meet the actual theorem conditions, rather than merely expose a stop command?; Assumption 9 and infinite-exploration/convergence conditions are material. The proof’s utility criterion must itself be acceptable.

<a id="eaa-042"></a>
### EAA-042 — Objective uncertainty as a conditional route to deference

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S040; DATE: 2017; WORK: The Off-Switch Game; VERSION: IJCAI 2017 pp.220–227; DOI 10.24963/ijcai.2017/32; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S041; DATE: 2025; WORK: The Partially Observable Off-Switch Game; VERSION: AAAI 2025 final proceedings PDF; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Utility uncertainty used to analyse off-switch incentives, later extended to private-information games.

**Development:** Retain the conditional incentive insight while rejecting its promotion to a universal corrigibility guarantee.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain the conditional incentive insight while rejecting its promotion to a universal corrigibility guarantee.; OPERATION: Represent uncertainty about the objective and the evidential value of human action, but determine deference under the actual information and interaction model rather than assuming uncertainty guarantees it.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: Explicit externally enforced authority can be sufficient for a bounded task; no Bayesian game is required to honour an ordinary stop rule.

**Problem:** A utility-maximising agent may have no incentive to preserve human intervention when it treats its objective as settled.

**Mechanism:** Represent uncertainty about the objective and the evidential value of human action, but determine deference under the actual information and interaction model rather than assuming uncertainty guarantees it.

**Trigger and context:** Designing incentives for decisions shared between a human and an uncertain-utility assistant.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: A defensible utility/observation model and a justified account of human information and choices.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Agent incentive designer and reviewer

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Agent incentive designer and reviewer; BOUNDARY: The relevant actor may act only through its real capability and current delegated scope. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. A defensible utility/observation model and a justified account of human information and choices.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Identify sender, recipient, consumer and non-response path; provide the task-relevant state needed for actual takeover. Consumer and prerequisite: Agent incentive designer and reviewer. A defensible utility/observation model and a justified account of human information and choices.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Any claimed deference result is stated with the game and assumptions that entail it.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** Explicit externally enforced authority can be sufficient for a bounded task; no Bayesian game is required to honour an ordinary stop rule.

**Historical disposition:** CONTESTED

**Failure modes:** The partially observable game supplies cases where a rational uncertain assistant does not defer; more communication need not increase deference monotonically.; Introduced cost or harm: Model complexity, misestimated human information and misplaced assurance.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: The partially observable game supplies cases where a rational uncertain assistant does not defer; more communication need not increase deference monotonically.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C008; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S041; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: The partially observable game supplies cases where a rational uncertain assistant does not defer; more communication need not increase deference monotonically.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S041; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Common payoff, private information, rationality and available actions substantially change the result.

**Unresolved questions:** Under the actual information asymmetry, does uncertainty promote deference or can it justify overriding the human?; Common payoff, private information, rationality and available actions substantially change the result.

Simplification is acceptable only while the cheaper path still establishes the relevant criterion.; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT_CONDITIONS: Do not adopt this record as an unconditional control. ### EAA-043 — User autonomy, privacy and affected-party accountability

**Historical origin:** ORIGINAL_OR_INSPECTED_ANCHORS: SOURCE_ID: EAA-S043; DATE: 1997; WORK: Software Agents and User Autonomy; VERSION: Proceedings of Autonomous Agents 1997, pp.466–469; SOURCE_ID: EAA-S021; DATE: undated author manuscript (workshop-era; exact year unverified); WORK: Electric Elves: What Went Wrong and Why; VERSION: Six-page author manuscript; distinct from the later 2008 AI Magazine article; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are the exact inspected anchors, not an unsupported assertion of unique priority or direct influence among all cited works.

**Original form:** Normative distinctions concerning capability, knowledge, misrepresentation and fluid control, with deployment privacy lessons.

**Development:** Retain meaningful participation and accountability as design constraints, not a claim that all ethical conflicts have a technical solution.

**Evolved form at historical freeze:** FORM: Retain meaningful participation and accountability as design constraints, not a claim that all ethical conflicts have a technical solution.; OPERATION: Identify whose choices, information and resources are affected; make material action and delegation understandable and contestable, with privacy and scope constraints applied to communication and memory.; SELECTION_OR_OMISSION: A minimal transparent fixed action can satisfy the need; avoid collecting or storing personal data with no task consumer.

**Problem:** An agent can advance a narrow task while reducing meaningful user control, exposing information or imposing unconsented costs on others.

**Mechanism:** Identify whose choices, information and resources are affected; make material action and delegation understandable and contestable, with privacy and scope constraints applied to communication and memory.

**Trigger and context:** Assistance or autonomous action affects people beyond internal computation.

**Preconditions:** CONDITIONS: Identifiable participants, intelligible consequences and an actual route for correction or limitation.; PROPERTY_IDS: []; RELATION_SEMANTICS: A REQUIRES edge is guarded; it does not require a dedicated module or owner for each referenced property.

**Decision or consumer:** Delegator, affected participant and accountable operator

**Authority boundary:** ACTOR_OR_CONSUMER: Delegator, affected participant and accountable operator; BOUNDARY: This is a research/evaluation criterion or rejected inference; it does not itself confer operative authority. Changing means does not authorise changing protected goals, user constraints or another participant’s rights. Identifiable participants, intelligible consequences and an actual route for correction or limitation.

**Communication or interaction:** FUNCTION: Identify sender, recipient, consumer and non-response path; provide the task-relevant state needed for actual takeover. Consumer and prerequisite: Delegator, affected participant and accountable operator. Identifiable participants, intelligible consequences and an actual route for correction or limitation.; LIMIT: No extra message, meeting or human approval ritual is required without a consequential recipient/decision. A sent request alone does not establish receipt, acceptance or action.

**Observable criterion:** Material effects are not hidden behind anthropomorphic assurances or a nominal user benefit, and relevant constraints can influence operation.

**Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path:** A minimal transparent fixed action can satisfy the need; avoid collecting or storing personal data with no task consumer.

**Historical disposition:** RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

**Failure modes:** More control knobs can reduce usable control; agent-centred autonomy can conflict with user autonomy, and a user’s wishes do not resolve every third-party claim.; Introduced cost or harm: Privacy protections, explanation effort, delayed assistance and conflicts among participants.

**Criticism:** SOURCE_GROUNDED_OBJECTION: More control knobs can reduce usable control; agent-centred autonomy can conflict with user autonomy, and a user’s wishes do not resolve every third-party claim.; CRITICISM_IDS: EAA-C007; EAA-C012; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S043; EAA-S021; ANALYTICAL_ADVERSARIAL_TEST: Challenge the payoff by the cheaper path, failed prerequisites, introduced cost and target-specific contrary evidence; do not count the mechanism as present merely because its artefact exists.

**Contrary evidence:** STATEMENT: More control knobs can reduce usable control; agent-centred autonomy can conflict with user autonomy, and a user’s wishes do not resolve every third-party claim.; SOURCE_IDS: EAA-S043; EAA-S021; IS_GENERAL_REFUTATION: false; LIMIT: A counterexample to a universal claim is not a population failure-rate estimate; non-conforming implementation failures do not logically refute a theorem under its assumptions.

**Assumption sensitivity:** Normative analysis is not a legal determination; local institutional and participant requirements remain to be established.

**Unresolved questions:** Whose autonomy or information is affected, and can those consequences be understood and appropriately constrained?; Normative analysis is not a legal determination; local institutional and participant requirements remain to be established.
